# AI and biosecurity: frameworks, evaluations, synthesis screening, government measures and arguments

Generated from `pack.json` by `node research/ai-bio/tools.mjs render`; do not edit by hand. Candidate research pack outside application data. Machine-prepared and audited by the same model family; not editorially accepted; no human review; cross-family check not yet run. Check date 2026-10-28. Method and limits: [README](README.md) and [scope](scope.md).

Each record states what a document says, dated by the kind of date the source gives. A signature, listing or framework reference is not compliance; a provision states what the text requires of the categories it defines, not that anyone is covered or complied; an announcement is recorded as announced.

| 2010-10-13 | published | Synthesis screening | The United States Department of Health and Human Services, through its Office of the Secretary, published the Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA in the Federal Register of 13 October 2010, stating that it sets out recommended baseline standards for the gene and genome synthesis industry and other providers regarding the screening of orders.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `ab-scr-hhs-2010-published`                                  |
| 2022-04-29 | published | Synthesis screening | The Administration for Strategic Preparedness and Response published a proposed revised screening framework guidance for providers and users of synthetic oligonucleotides in the Federal Register of 29 April 2022, submitting it for public consideration and comment for a period of 60 days.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `ab-scr-hhs-2022-draft-published`                            |
| 2023-06-06 | published | Arguments           | The preprint "Can large language models democratize access to dual-use biotechnology?" (arXiv v1, 6 June 2023) states that large language models will make pandemic-class agents widely accessible even to people with little or no laboratory training.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `ab-arg-b1-soice-articulates`                                |
| 2023-06-06 | published | Arguments           | The preprint "Can large language models democratize access to dual-use biotechnology?" (arXiv v1, 6 June 2023) states that the agents it discusses would become widely accessible as soon as they are credibly identified, even to people with little or no laboratory training, against the premise that laboratory execution is the binding barrier.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `ab-arg-b2-soice-contests`                                   |
| 2023-06-06 | published | Arguments           | The preprint of 6 June 2023 states that promising nonproliferation measures include pre-release evaluations of language models by third parties, curation of training datasets, and verifiable screening of all DNA generated by synthesis providers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `ab-arg-b5-soice-articulates`                                |
| 2023-06-24 | published | Arguments           | Sandbrink's preprint on AI and biological misuse (arXiv v1, 24 June 2023) states that large language models such as GPT-4 are already able to provide dual-use information that could have enabled historical biological weapons efforts to succeed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `ab-arg-b1-sandbrink-articulates`                            |
| 2023-06-24 | published | Arguments           | Sandbrink's preprint (arXiv v1, 24 June 2023) differentiates large language models from biological design tools, stating that language models in particular lower barriers to biological misuse while design tools expand the capabilities of sophisticated actors.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `ab-arg-b4-sandbrink-articulates`                            |
| 2023-06-24 | published | Arguments           | Sandbrink's preprint (arXiv v1, 24 June 2023) states that enhanced screening of gene synthesis is among the measures key to reducing the risks it describes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `ab-arg-b5-sandbrink-articulates`                            |
| 2023-06-24 | published | Arguments           | Sandbrink's preprint (arXiv v1, 24 June 2023) states that biological design tools will expand the capabilities of sophisticated actors, and proposes interventions to deter biological misuse by such actors.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `ab-arg-b7-sandbrink-articulates`                            |

### Responsible Scaling Policy (2023)

#### What the text states

- **RSP v1.0 illustrates its ASL-2 level with an AI system that can provide bioweapon-related information that could not be found via a search engine, but does so too unreliably to be useful in practice.** `ab-fw-rsp-2023-asl2-bio-information-level`
  2023-09-19 · effective
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s effective date.
  _Limits:_ The record states how the edition illustrates a capability level. It is not a finding about any model, and the edition’s own ASL-2 row describes an example, not a measured capability.

  > “an AI system that can provide bioweapon-related information that couldn’t be found via a search engine, but does so too unreliably to be useful in practice”  
  > [Responsible Scaling Policy — v1.0](https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/1adf000c8f675958c2ee23805d91aaade1cd4613/responsible-scaling-policy.pdf), PDF p. 4 (printed p. 4), AI Safety Level table, ASL-2 row, “Dangerous Capabilities” column

- **RSP v1.0 states that the misuse domains covered by its ASL-3 deployment red-teaming measure should at a minimum include causes of extreme CBRN risks, and cybersecurity.** `ab-fw-rsp-2023-asl3-redteam-cbrn-domains`
  2023-09-19 · effective
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s effective date.
  _Limits:_ A stated requirement of the edition. It is not evidence that red-teaming was performed, nor that it would detect anything.

  > “Misuse domains should at a minimum include causes of extreme CBRN risks, and cybersecurity.”  
  > [Responsible Scaling Policy — v1.0](https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/1adf000c8f675958c2ee23805d91aaade1cd4613/responsible-scaling-policy.pdf), PDF p. 8 (printed p. 8), “ASL-3 Deployment Measures”, bullet “Successfully pass red-teaming”, second sentence

- **RSP v1.0 states, as an early guess at what ASL-4 will involve, a critical catastrophic misuse risk in which AI models have become the primary source of national security risk in a major area such as cyberattacks or biological weapons, rather than a significant contributor.** `ab-fw-rsp-2023-asl4-anticipated-misuse-level`
  2023-09-19 · effective
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s effective date.
  _Limits:_ The edition presents this as speculative and likely to change; the record states what the edition guesses, not a defined threshold or any capability of any model.

  > “AI models have become the primary source of national security risk in a major area (such as cyberattacks or biological weapons)”  
  > [Responsible Scaling Policy — v1.0](https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/1adf000c8f675958c2ee23805d91aaade1cd4613/responsible-scaling-policy.pdf), PDF p. 14 (printed p. 14), “Early Thoughts on ASL-4 and Higher”, bullet “Critical catastrophic misuse risk”, first sentence

- **RSP v1.0 states that Anthropic’s first area of effort is evaluating bioweapons risks, for which it will determine threat models and capabilities in consultation with a number of world-class biosecurity experts.** `ab-fw-rsp-2023-bio-evaluation-first-area`
  2023-09-19 · effective · adds to dataset record `cmp-rsp-2023-d1-cbrn`
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s effective date.
  _Limits:_ A stated intention in the edition. It is not evidence that any evaluation was built, run or completed, and it names no expert or organisation.

  > “Our first area of effort is in evaluating bioweapons risks where we will determine threat models and capabilities in consultation with a number of world-class biosecurity experts.”  
  > [Responsible Scaling Policy — v1.0](https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/1adf000c8f675958c2ee23805d91aaade1cd4613/responsible-scaling-policy.pdf), PDF p. 7 (printed p. 7), section “ASL-3 Capabilities and Threat Models”, item 1, paragraph following the misuse threshold

- **RSP v1.0 states, as an example of its tiered-access measure, that potentially harmful biology capabilities that could be used for cancer research might be made available to a small group of vetted researchers at organisations committing to strong, well defined and thoroughly vetted security and internal controls.** `ab-fw-rsp-2023-tiered-access-biology-exception`
  2023-09-19 · effective
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s effective date.
  _Limits:_ A stated exception in the edition, given as an example. It records no grant of access to anyone and names no organisation.
  > The bullet says that, in limited cases, models with capabilities relevant to catastrophic harm may be made available to a select group of vetted users with a legitimate and beneficial use case that cannot be separated from dangerous capabilities, and gives as its example potentially harmful biology capabilities usable for cancer research being made available to a small group of vetted researchers at organisations that commit to strong, well defined and thoroughly vetted security and internal controls. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Responsible Scaling Policy — v1.0](https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/1adf000c8f675958c2ee23805d91aaade1cd4613/responsible-scaling-policy.pdf), PDF p. 9 (printed p. 9), “ASL-3 Deployment Measures”, bullet “Tiered access”, example sentence

### OpenAI Preparedness Framework — Beta

#### What the text states

- **The Preparedness Framework (Beta) defines its CBRN tracked risk category as focused on risks related to model-assisted creation of chemical, biological, radiological and/or nuclear threats.** `ab-fw-pf-beta-cbrn-category-definition`
  2023-12-18 · published · adds to dataset record `cmp-preparedness-beta-2023-d1-cbrn`
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s publication date.
  _Limits:_ The record states how the edition defines a tracked category. It is not a finding about any model.

  > “CBRN is focused on risks related to model-assisted creation of chemical, biological, radiological, and/or nuclear threats.”  
  > [Preparedness Framework — Beta](https://cdn.openai.com/openai-preparedness-framework-beta.pdf), PDF p. 9 (printed p. 9), Tracked Risk Categories, “CBRN (chemical, biological, radiological, nuclear)”, opening sentence

- **The Preparedness Framework (Beta) defines the High risk level of its CBRN category as a model that enables an expert to develop a novel threat vector, or provides meaningfully improved assistance enabling anyone with basic training in a relevant field to create a CBRN threat.** `ab-fw-pf-beta-cbrn-high-level`
  2023-12-18 · published
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s publication date.
  _Limits:_ The record states the risk-level definition as the edition words it, at the generality of its own table cell. It is not a finding about any model.
  > The cell reads that the model enables an expert to develop a novel threat vector, or provides meaningfully improved assistance that enables anyone with basic training in a relevant field, its example being an introductory undergraduate biology course, to create a CBRN threat. _(paraphrase)_

### Frontier Safety Framework (2024)

### Google DeepMind Frontier Safety Framework — v3.0

#### Versions and changes

- **The Frontier Safety Framework v3.1 change notes state that the update raises the recommended level of security for the CBRN, Cyber and Harmful Manipulation Critical Capability Levels to Security Level 2+.** `ab-fw-fsf-v3-1-cbrn-security-level-2-plus`
  2026-04-17 · published
  _Date:_ Change as stated in the v3.1 change notes, dated by the edition’s publication date.
  _Limits:_ The publisher’s own description of its change. It is not evidence that any level of security was applied, and the edition does not say what Security Level 2+ requires beyond Security level 2.
  > The bullet says the version outlined an enhanced level of security for the CBRN, Cyber and Harmful Manipulation Critical Capability Levels to Security Level 2+. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Frontier Safety Framework — v3.1](https://storage.googleapis.com/deepmind-media/DeepMind.com/Blog/strengthening-our-frontier-safety-framework/frontier-safety-framework_3-1.pdf), PDF p. 17 (printed p. 17), §5.3 Past Updates and Changes, “Version 3.1 (April 17, 2026)”, third bullet
  > The v3.0 column recommends Security level 2 for CBRN uplift level 1. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Frontier Safety Framework — v3.0](https://storage.googleapis.com/deepmind-media/DeepMind.com/Blog/strengthening-our-frontier-safety-framework/frontier-safety-framework_3.pdf), PDF p. 10 (printed p. 10), Table 2.2.1.a, “Recommended security level and rationale” column

#### What the text states

- **The Frontier Safety Framework v3.0 recommends Security level 2 for its CBRN uplift level 1 Critical Capability Level, reasoning that the difficulty of building defences against certain CBRN threats makes exfiltration and leak of such model weights highly damaging, while the actors most likely to gain uplift are unlikely to pose an exfiltration threat at the level of RAND OC3 groups.** `ab-fw-fsf-v3-0-cbrn-uplift-1-security-level`
  2025-09-22 · published
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s publication date.
  _Limits:_ A recommendation and its stated reasoning. It is not evidence that the level was applied, nor an assessment of whether the reasoning holds.

  > The column recommends Security level 2 and gives as its rationale that the difficulty of building defences against certain CBRN threats means the exfiltration and leak of model weights with this capability could be highly damaging, but that the low to medium resourced actors likely to experience the most CBRN uplift are unlikely to pose a substantial exfiltration threat at the level of RAND OC3 groups. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Frontier Safety Framework — v3.0](https://storage.googleapis.com/deepmind-media/DeepMind.com/Blog/strengthening-our-frontier-safety-framework/frontier-safety-framework_3.pdf), PDF p. 10 (printed p. 10), Table 2.2.1.a, “Recommended security level and rationale” column

- **The Frontier Safety Framework v3.0 states a misuse Critical Capability Level named “CBRN uplift level 1”: providing low to medium resourced actors uplift in reference scenarios resulting in additional expected harm at severe scale.** `ab-fw-fsf-v3-0-cbrn-uplift-level-1-threshold`
  2025-09-22 · published
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s publication date.
  _Limits:_ The record states the capability level as the edition words it. The edition does not publish its reference scenarios, and the record is not a finding about any model.
  > The column reads that CBRN uplift level 1 provides low to medium resourced actors uplift in reference scenarios resulting in additional expected harm at severe scale, with a footnote marker after “additional” that the edition glosses as meaning relative to a baseline without generative AI. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Frontier Safety Framework — v3.0](https://storage.googleapis.com/deepmind-media/DeepMind.com/Blog/strengthening-our-frontier-safety-framework/frontier-safety-framework_3.pdf), PDF p. 10 (printed p. 10), Table 2.2.1.a: CBRN CCLs and Security Mitigations, “Critical Capability Level” column

Risk Assessment”, paragraph introducing the restricted critical steps

- **The Frontier Artificial Intelligence Framework of December 2025 states that, without any safeguards, xAI recognises that advanced AI models could lower the barrier to entry for bad actors seeking to develop CBRN or cyber weapons and could help automate knowledge compilation to overcome bottlenecks to weapons development.** `ab-fw-faif-2025-cbrn-barrier-recognition`
  2025-12-30 · revision · adds to dataset record `cmp-faif-2025-d1-cbrn`
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s revision date.

  > “advanced AI models could lower the barrier to entry for bad actors seeking to develop chemical, biological, radiological, or nuclear (“CBRN”) or cyber weapons, and could help automate knowledge compilation to swiftly overcome bottlenecks to weapons development”  
  > [Frontier Artificial Intelligence Framework — December 2025 edition](https://data.x.ai/2025-12-31-xai-frontier-artificial-intelligence-framework.pdf), PDF p. 3 (no printed page numbers), “Addressing Risks of Malicious Use”, first paragraph, second sentence

- **The Frontier Artificial Intelligence Framework of December 2025 states that xAI’s models apply heightened safeguards if they receive user prompts posing a foreseeable and non-trivial risk of resulting in large-scale violence, terrorism, or the use, development or proliferation of weapons of mass destruction, including CBRN weapons, and major cyber attacks on critical infrastructure.** `ab-fw-faif-2025-heightened-safeguards`
  2025-12-30 · revision · adds to dataset record `prov-faif-2026-heightened-safeguards`
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s revision date.

  > [Frontier Artificial Intelligence Framework — December 2025 edition](https://data.x.ai/2025-12-31-xai-frontier-artificial-intelligence-framework.pdf), PDF p. 3 (no printed page numbers), “Addressing Risks of Malicious Use”, second paragraph, first sentence

- **The Frontier Artificial Intelligence Framework of December 2025 states that xAI may selectively allow its models to respond to requests that heightened safeguards would otherwise restrict, where the requester is among some vetted, highly trusted users known to be using those capabilities for benign or beneficial purposes.** `ab-fw-faif-2025-vetted-user-exception`
  2025-12-30 · revision
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s revision date.

### Anthropic Responsible Scaling Policy — v3.0

#### What the text states

- **RSP v3.0 recommends, for any frontier developer at its non-novel chemical/biological weapons production threshold, a strong argument that individual users and relatively small teams will not become significantly more likely to cause catastrophic harm through use of product surfaces or theft of model weights.** `ab-fw-rsp-v3-0-non-novel-cb-industry-recommendation`
  2026-02-24 · effective

  _Limits:_ A recommendation the edition addresses to the industry, not a requirement on anyone and not a finding that any developer meets it.

  > The column says a frontier developer should make a strong argument that individual users and relatively small teams will not become significantly more likely to cause catastrophic harm via their usage of product surfaces or via theft of model weights. _(paraphrase)_

- **RSP v3.0 states that, at its novel chemical/biological weapons production threshold, Anthropic will apply protections at least as strong as its ASL-3 protections to an expanded set of potential use cases for AI, covering what it calls the most likely vectors for this threat.** `ab-fw-rsp-v3-0-novel-cb-mitigations`
  2026-02-24 · effective

  > The column says Anthropic will apply protections at least as strong as its ASL-3 protections, described in the previous row, to an expanded set of potential use cases for AI, covering the most likely vectors for this threat. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Responsible Scaling Policy — v3.0](https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/e670587677525f28df69b59e5fb4c22cc5461a17.pdf), PDF p. 7 (printed p. 7), §1, threshold table, row “Novel chemical/biological weapons production”, middle column, first paragraph

- **RSP v3.0 states that, for its novel chemical/biological weapons production threshold, Anthropic will identify the most concerning specific threat pathways, create policy recommendations for early detection and response, and share this content with policymakers.** `ab-fw-rsp-v3-0-novel-cb-threat-pathway-sharing`
  2026-02-24 · effective
  _Date:_ Provision as stated in this edition, dated by the edition’s effective date.
  _Limits:_ A stated commitment of the edition. Nothing in the record says that any pathway was identified, any recommendation made or anything shared; no pathway is named.
  > “we will identify the most concerning specific threat pathways, create policy recommendations for early detection and response for such threats, and share this content with policymakers”  
  > [Responsible Scaling Policy — v3.0](https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/e670587677525f28df69b59e5fb4c22cc5461a17.pdf), PDF p. 7 (printed p. 7), §1 Our Recommendations for Industry-Wide Safety, threshold table, row “Novel chemical/biological weapons production”, middle column (“Mitigations—our plan as a company”), second paragraph

### The Operational Risks of AI in Large-Scale Biological Attacks: Results of a Red-Team Study

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **RAND's red-team study states that it found no statistically significant difference in the viability of the plans produced by its red cells with or without large language model assistance, for the two models and the fifteen cells it describes.** `ab-ev-rand-redteam-no-difference`
  2024-01-25 · published · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The result as published on 25 January 2024; the report describes the models as those existing as of summer 2023.
  _Limits:_ The result is the study's own, for the two unnamed models as they existed in summer 2023, its participants, its scoring and its vignettes. The report itself lists reasons other than model capability that could account for the absence of a difference, and states that it did not measure the distance between the existing capability frontier and the knowledge that would be needed. A null result here is not evidence that no uplift exists, and nothing is generalised to later models.

  > “We conclude that biological weapon attack planning lies beyond the existing capability frontier of LLMs.”  
  > [The Operational Risks of AI in Large-Scale Biological Attacks: Results of a Red-Team Study (RR-A2977-2)](https://www.rand.org/content/dam/rand/pubs/research_reports/RRA2900/RRA2977-2/RAND_RRA2977-2.pdf), PDF p. 2, 'We conclude that…' passage in the introductory column
  > “there was no significant difference in the viability of OPLANs created with or without the aid of LLMs”  
  > [The Operational Risks of AI in Large-Scale Biological Attacks: Results of a Red-Team Study (RR-A2977-2)](https://www.rand.org/content/dam/rand/pubs/research_reports/RRA2900/RRA2977-2/RAND_RRA2977-2.pdf), PDF p. 8, analysis of the combined results and of the two models
  > “Several factors beyond the capabilities or limitations of LLMs could account for this lack of statistically significant difference between the LLM and internet-only cells.”  
  > [The Operational Risks of AI in Large-Scale Biological Attacks: Results of a Red-Team Study (RR-A2977-2)](https://www.rand.org/content/dam/rand/pubs/research_reports/RRA2900/RRA2977-2/RAND_RRA2977-2.pdf), PDF p. 8, paragraph following the difference-in-means analysis · context

- **RAND published 'The Operational Risks of AI in Large-Scale Biological Attacks: Results of a Red-Team Study' (RR-A2977-2) on 25 January 2024.** `ab-ev-rand-redteam-published`
  2024-01-25 · published
  _Date:_ Date stated on RAND's publication page for RR-A2977-2; the report PDF carries only a 2024 copyright line.
  _Limits:_ Publication of the report is the record. An earlier RAND document of October 2023 described the study's approach; it was not retrieved here.
  > “Published Jan 25, 2024”  
  > [RAND publication page for RR-A2977-2 (publication date)](https://www.rand.org/pubs/research_reports/RRA2977-2.html), Publication page header for RR-A2977-2
  > The report is titled 'The Operational Risks of AI in Large-Scale Biological Attacks: Results of a Red-Team Study', is credited to Christopher A. Mouton, Caleb Lucas and Ella Guest, and carries a 2024 RAND Corporation copyright line and the number RR-A2977-2. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [The Operational Risks of AI in Large-Scale Biological Attacks: Results of a Red-Team Study (RR-A2977-2)](https://www.rand.org/content/dam/rand/pubs/research_reports/RRA2900/RRA2977-2/RAND_RRA2977-2.pdf), PDF p. 1, title block, and p. 24, copyright block

  > [arXiv abstract page for arXiv:2403.03218 (The WMDP Benchmark: Measuring and Reducing Malicious Use With Unlearning)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03218), Abstract, fifth sentence

### Advanced AI evaluations at AISI: May update

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **The UK institute's May 2024 evaluations update states that several of the five publicly available large language models it tested demonstrated expert-level knowledge of chemistry and biology, answering over 600 private expert-written chemistry and biology questions at levels similar to humans with PhD-level training.** `ab-ev-uk-aisi-may2024-chembio-result`
  2024-05-20 · published · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The result as published on 20 May 2024; the models are anonymised in the post.
  _Limits:_ The result is the institute's own, for five models it does not name, its private question set and its stated human comparison. The post states that its work does not provide any assurance that a model is safe or unsafe, and that there may be a gap between evaluation performance and behaviour in the wild. Nothing is generalised to other models or to real-world capability. The expression checked is the copy served on 2026-10-28, which is later than first publication: it carries a note to readers that the institute changed its name to the AI Security Institute on 14 February 2025, and the address redirects to /blog/advanced-ai-evaluations-may-update. The record keeps the byline date of 20 May 2024, and the quoted wording was read in that served copy.

  > “Several LLMs demonstrated expert-level knowledge of chemistry and biology. Models answered over 600 private expert-written chemistry and biology questions at similar levels to humans with PhD-level training.”  
  > [Advanced AI evaluations at AISI: May update](https://www.aisi.gov.uk/work/advanced-ai-evaluations-may-update), 'Here, we highlight a selection of recent results' list, first item
  > “Our work does not provide any assurance that a model is "safe" or "unsafe".”  
  > [Advanced AI evaluations at AISI: May update](https://www.aisi.gov.uk/work/advanced-ai-evaluations-may-update), 'Outlook' section, final paragraph · context
  > “we remain acutely aware of the potential gap between how advanced AI systems perform in our evaluations versus how they may perform in the wild”  
  > [Advanced AI evaluations at AISI: May update](https://www.aisi.gov.uk/work/advanced-ai-evaluations-may-update), 'Outlook' section, penultimate paragraph · context

- **The UK institute, then named the AI Safety Institute, published 'Advanced AI evaluations at AISI: May update' on 20 May 2024, stating that it had tested leading AI models for cyber, chemical, biological and agent capabilities and for safeguard effectiveness.** `ab-ev-uk-aisi-may2024-published`
  2024-05-20 · published
  _Date:_ Byline date on the post ('Technical staff — May 20, 2024').
  _Limits:_ Publication of the update is not evidence that the evaluations were adequate or complete. The institute was renamed in February 2025; the publishing body is recorded under its dataset identity with the name used at the time noted.
  > “We tested leading AI models for cyber, chemical, biological, and agent capabilities and safeguards effectiveness.”  
  > [Advanced AI evaluations at AISI: May update](https://www.aisi.gov.uk/work/advanced-ai-evaluations-may-update), Page title block and byline

### The Llama 3 Herd of Models (arXiv:2407.21783)

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **The Llama 3 paper states that its uplift testing for chemical and biological weapons found no significant uplift in performance related to use of the Llama 3 model, both in an aggregate comparison with a web-only control condition and in its subgroup breakdowns.** `ab-ev-llama3-cb-uplift-result`
  2024-11-23 · published · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ As stated in version 3 of the paper; the study itself is not dated in the passage read.
  _Limits:_ The result is the paper's own, for the model versions, participants and scoring it describes, against a web-only control. It is not generalised to other systems, later versions or real-world outcomes, and a null result in this study is not evidence that no uplift exists.

  > “To assess risks related to proliferation of chemical and biological weapons, we perform uplift testing designed to assess whether use of Llama 3 could meaningfully increase the capabilities of actors to plan such attacks.”  
  > [The Llama 3 Herd of Models (arXiv:2407.21783v3)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.21783v3), PDF p. 47, §5.4.5, 'Uplift testing for chemical and biological weapons'
  > “Quantitative analysis of these results of this study show no significant uplift in performance related to usage of the Llama 3 model.”  
  > [The Llama 3 Herd of Models (arXiv:2407.21783v3)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.21783v3), PDF pp. 47-48, §5.4.5, results paragraph

- **The Llama 3 authors at Meta published 'The Llama 3 Herd of Models' as arXiv:2407.21783, first submitted on 31 July 2024, with the version read here submitted on 23 November 2024.** `ab-ev-llama3-paper-published`
  2024-11-23 · published
  _Date:_ Version 3, the expression read. Version 1 was submitted on 31 July 2024, recorded in the document entry as the first publication date.
  _Limits:_ Publication of the paper is not evidence that the evaluations it reports were carried out as described. The paper is a preprint; no peer-reviewed version was sought.

  > The abstract page lists version 1 submitted on 31 July 2024, version 2 on 15 August 2024 and version 3 on 23 November 2024, which is the current version. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [arXiv abstract page for arXiv:2407.21783 (The Llama 3 Herd of Models), submission history](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21783), Submission history block of the arXiv abstract page
  > The paper is titled 'The Llama 3 Herd of Models' and is credited to the Llama 3 team at Meta. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [The Llama 3 Herd of Models (arXiv:2407.21783v3)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.21783v3), PDF p. 1, title and author credit

- **The Llama 3 paper states that, after validating its uplift results with CBRNE subject matter experts, it assesses that there is a low risk that release of the Llama 3 models will increase ecosystem risk related to biological or chemical weapon attacks.** `ab-ev-llama3-risk-assessment`
  2024-11-23 · published · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ As stated in version 3 of the paper.
  _Limits:_ An assessment by the developers of the models released, based on their own study; it is recorded as their statement, not adopted. It concerns the models released in 2024 and is not evidence about later models or about weights released afterwards.
  > “After validating these results with CBRNE SMEs, we assess that there is a low risk that release of Llama 3 models will increase ecosystem risk related to biological or chemical weapon attacks.”  
  > [The Llama 3 Herd of Models (arXiv:2407.21783v3)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.21783v3), PDF p. 48, §5.4.5, closing sentence

### OpenAI o1 System Card

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **The o1 System Card states that its evaluations found o1 can help experts with the operational planning of reproducing a known biological threat, which it says meets OpenAI's medium risk threshold, and that the Safety Advisory Group classified the pre-mitigation model as medium risk for CBRN.** `ab-ev-o1-card-cb-medium-result`
  2024-12-05 · published · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The classification as stated in the card expression of 5 December 2024. The expression checked is the PDF served at cdn.openai.com/o1-system-card-20241205.pdf on 2026-10-28, which carries a footnote in §3 ('Scope of testing') reading 'Section added after December 5th on 12/19/2024'; the served file is therefore a later expression than the card's stated date of 5 December 2024, the card does not say what else differs, and the record keeps the stated date.
  _Limits:_ A risk classification stated by the developer for the model version it tested, under its own framework and thresholds. It is not an independent finding, is not generalised to other models or later versions, and says nothing about whether any safeguard works. The card adds that the exact performance numbers for the production model may vary.

  > “Our evaluations found that o1 can help experts with the operational planning of reproducing a known biological threat, which meets our medium risk threshold.”

  > “the Safety Advisory Group … classified the o1 pre-mitigation model as overall medium risk, including medium risk for persuasion and CBRN, and low risk for model autonomy and cybersecurity.”

The expression checked is the PDF served at cdn.openai.com/o1-system-card-20241205.pdf on 2026-10-28, which carries a footnote in §3 ('Scope of testing') reading 'Section added after December 5th on 12/19/2024'; the served file is therefore a later expression than the card's stated date of 5 December 2024, the card does not say what else differs, and the record keeps the stated date.

  2025-08-13 · published

  _Limits:_ Reported performed work, as the developer describes it. The institute's own report was not retrieved, and the record does not establish the scope or depth of the testing. This record cannot be admitted as structured without a maintainer decision: `evaluation_engagement` admits `organisation` subjects only (`src/domain/predicates.ts`, enforced by the schema's subject-type check), while the subject `uk-ai-security-institute` is typed `public_body` in `data/entities.json`, so admission as recorded would fail validation. The pack does not change the dataset or the schema, so the record is left as it stands and the question is raised in `openQuestions`.
  > “Both CAISI and UK AISI conducted evaluations of the model's cyber and biological and chemical capabilities, as well as safeguards.”  
  > [GPT-5 System Card](https://cdn.openai.com/gpt-5-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 53 (printed p. 52), §5.3.3.5 External government red teaming

  > [GPT-5 System Card](https://cdn.openai.com/gpt-5-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 53 (printed p. 52), §5.3.3.5

### Virology Capabilities Test (VCT): A Multimodal Virology Q&A Benchmark (arXiv:2504.16137)

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **The Virology Capabilities Test paper states that it presents a large language model benchmark measuring the capability to troubleshoot complex virology laboratory protocols, built from the inputs of dozens of expert virologists.** `ab-ev-vct-benchmark-measures`
  2025-04-29 · published
  _Date:_ Version 2 of the preprint, as described on the arXiv abstract page; version 1 was submitted on 21 April 2025.
  _Limits:_ Records what the benchmark states it measures, as described in its abstract, not any finding about a system. Only the abstract page was read; the paper's construction and contents were not inspected.

  > “a large language model (LLM) benchmark that measures the capability to troubleshoot complex virology laboratory protocols”  
  > [arXiv abstract page for arXiv:2504.16137 (Virology Capabilities Test (VCT): A Multimodal Virology Q&A Benchmark)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16137), Abstract, first two sentences

- **The Virology Capabilities Test paper states that expert virologists with internet access scored an average of 22.1% on the benchmark's questions within their own sub-areas of expertise, while the most performant model it tested, OpenAI's o3, reached 43.8% accuracy and outperformed 94% of those experts within their sub-areas.** `ab-ev-vct-benchmark-result`
  2025-04-29 · published · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ As stated in version 2 of the preprint; the dates of the model evaluations are not given on the abstract page.
  _Limits:_ A benchmark result as the paper states it, for the models and expert comparison group it describes at that date. Scores on this benchmark are not a measure of real-world capability or risk, and nothing is generalised to other models or later versions. Only the abstract page was read, so the figures are the paper's own summary.
  > The abstract states that expert virologists with access to the internet score an average of 22.1% on questions in their own sub-areas of expertise, and that the most performant model tested, OpenAI's o3, reaches 43.8% accuracy, outperforming 94% of expert virologists even within their sub-areas of specialisation. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [arXiv abstract page for arXiv:2504.16137 (Virology Capabilities Test (VCT): A Multimodal Virology Q&A Benchmark)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16137), Abstract, third and fourth sentences

### Activating AI Safety Level 3 protections

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **Anthropic's ASL-3 activation post states that it had not yet determined whether Claude Opus 4 definitively passed the capabilities threshold requiring ASL-3 protections, that continued improvements in CBRN-related knowledge and capabilities meant clearly ruling out ASL-3 risks was not possible as it had been for every previous model, and that it deployed the model with ASL-3 measures as a precautionary and provisional action.** `ab-ev-anthropic-asl3-opus4-determination`
  2025-05-22 · published · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The determination as stated on 22 May 2025.
  _Limits:_ A developer's precautionary decision under its own policy, stated as provisional; the post says the protections may be removed or adjusted if it later concludes the threshold was not surpassed. It is not an independent finding about the model's capability and says nothing about whether the measures work.

  > “we have not yet determined whether Claude Opus 4 has definitively passed the Capabilities Threshold that requires ASL-3 protections”  
  > [Activating AI Safety Level 3 protections](https://www.anthropic.com/news/activating-asl3-protections), First section, second paragraph
  > The post states that, because of continued improvements in CBRN-related knowledge and capabilities, clearly ruling out ASL-3 risks was not possible for Claude Opus 4 in the way it had been for every previous model, and that more detailed study is required. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Activating AI Safety Level 3 protections](https://www.anthropic.com/news/activating-asl3-protections), First section, second paragraph

- **Anthropic published 'Activating AI Safety Level 3 protections' on 22 May 2025, stating that it had activated the ASL-3 Deployment and Security Standards of its Responsible Scaling Policy in conjunction with launching Claude Opus 4.** `ab-ev-anthropic-asl3-published`
  2025-05-22 · published
  _Date:_ Date stated on the page.
  _Limits:_ The record is the announcement. It is not evidence that the measures described were implemented as described or that they are effective. The same page states that deploying Claude Opus 4 under the ASL-3 measures is 'a precautionary and provisional action' and that Anthropic had 'not yet determined whether Claude Opus 4 has definitively passed the Capabilities Threshold that requires ASL-3 protections', so the activation is not a finding that a capability threshold was crossed; the post adds that the protections may be removed or adjusted if it later concludes the threshold was not surpassed.

  > “We have activated the AI Safety Level 3 (ASL-3) Deployment and Security Standards described in Anthropic's Responsible Scaling Policy (RSP) in conjunction with launching Claude Opus 4.”  
  > [Activating AI Safety Level 3 protections](https://www.anthropic.com/news/activating-asl3-protections), Page header and first paragraph
  > “We are deploying Claude Opus 4 with our ASL-3 measures as a precautionary and provisional action.”  
  > [Activating AI Safety Level 3 protections](https://www.anthropic.com/news/activating-asl3-protections), First section, second paragraph · context

- **Anthropic's ASL-3 activation post states that experiments showed access to Claude Sonnet 3.7 helped participants do somewhat better on tasks related to CBRN weapon acquisition than those with standard internet access, and that all participants' plans still had critical failures.** `ab-ev-anthropic-asl3-sonnet37-uplift`
  2025-05-22 · published · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ As stated in the post of 22 May 2025, reporting experiments it does not date.
  _Limits:_ A comparative result as the developer reports it in a footnote, for the model and the participant tasks it describes, with the post's own statement that all plans still had critical failures. It is not generalised beyond those experiments and does not quantify any uplift.
  > “Experiments showed that access to Claude Sonnet 3.7 helped participants do somewhat better on tasks related to CBRN weapon acquisition than those with standard internet access (although all participants' plans still had critical failures).”  
  > [Activating AI Safety Level 3 protections](https://www.anthropic.com/news/activating-asl3-protections), Footnote 2

#### What the text states

- **Anthropic's ASL-3 activation post states that the new ASL-3 deployment measures are initially focused exclusively on biological weapons, which it says account for the vast majority of the risk, and that an expansion in scope to some other CBRN threats is being evaluated.** `ab-ev-anthropic-asl3-bio-scope`
  2025-05-22 · published
  _Date:_ The stated scope as of the post of 22 May 2025.
  _Limits:_ The stated scope of announced measures in a safeguards post, not a provision of a framework edition and not evidence that the measures were applied or are effective.
  > “Initially they are focused exclusively on biological weapons as we believe these account for the vast majority of the risk”  
  > [Activating AI Safety Level 3 protections](https://www.anthropic.com/news/activating-asl3-protections), Footnote 3, attached to the Deployment Measures section

### Transparency hub (Anthropic), as served on 28 October 2026

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **Anthropic's transparency hub, as served on 28 October 2026, states that it deployed Claude Sonnet 5.5 with the same chemical and biological misuse protections as Claude Opus 5, treating it as meeting its CB-1 threshold for models able to significantly help someone with a basic technical background make known chemical or biological weapons.** `ab-ev-anthropic-hub-sonnet55-cb1`
  2026-10-28 · undefined · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The page states no publication or revision date; the record is the snapshot retrieved on 2026-10-28.
  _Limits:_ A summary page, not the system card itself, and a mutable one: the wording may change without notice, and the record is bounded to the snapshot read. It is the developer's own threshold treatment, not an independent finding, and not evidence that the protections work.
  > “On chemical and biological weapons, we treat Claude Sonnet 5.5 as able to significantly help someone with a basic technical background make known chemical or biological weapons, which is our CB-1 threshold.”  
  > [Transparency hub (Anthropic), model-by-model safety summaries](https://www.anthropic.com/transparency), Claude Sonnet 5.5 section, 'Testing Methods and Results' and the chemical and biological summary

### Kimi-K2-Thinking model card (README.md, main branch as served 28 October 2026)

- **No passage naming a biological, CBRN or biosecurity capability evaluation was found in the Kimi-K2-Thinking model card as served on 28 October 2026, within the whole of that file.** `ab-ev-kimi-k2t-no-bio-evaluation`
  2026-10-28 · undefined · Inferred
  _Date:_ The expression inspected is the main-branch README.md retrieved on 2026-10-28; the file states no date.
  _Reasoning:_ The retrieved file was searched in full, case-insensitively, for 'biolog', 'bio', 'CBRN', 'biosecur' and 'weapon'; no match occurred outside unrelated words. The absence is recorded for this expression and these terms only.
  _Limits:_ A recorded absence bounded to one file and one retrieval date. It is not evidence that the developer ran no such evaluation, that none is reported elsewhere, or that other non-US developers' cards are silent; the dataset's kimi-k2-thinking-card entity cites an earlier commit, which was not inspected here.

### Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **The Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card states that ASL-2 safeguards remain appropriate for the model, that its evaluations suggest improved performance in all domains including some uplift in CBRN evaluations, and that on what it observed in recent CBRN testing there is a substantial probability that the next model may require ASL-3 safeguards.** `ab-ev-claude37-asl2-determination`
  undated · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The card states no date; the determination is recorded as the card's, without a date.
  _Limits:_ A developer's determination under its own policy, for the model and evaluations the card describes. The stated expectation about a next model is the card's own statement about the future, not a recorded event. The card also states that some specific CBRN thresholds and results are treated as sensitive and not published, so the published basis is incomplete.

  > “RSP safeguards required for Claude 3.7 Sonnet: ASL-2”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 23, §7 RSP Evaluations, heading line
  > “the results from our evaluations suggest improved performance in all domains, including some uplift in CBRN evaluations”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 24, §7.1 CBRN Evaluations, conclusion paragraph
  > “based on what we observed in our recent CBRN testing, we believe there is a substantial probability that our next model may require ASL-3 safeguards”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 24, §7.1, conclusion paragraph
  > “Under our RSP framework, we consider some of the specific evaluation thresholds and results in the CBRN domain sensitive, and therefore we do not share them publicly.”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 23, §7.1, 'Threshold and evaluations' paragraph · context

- **Anthropic published the Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card, which states that its Responsible Scaling Policy evaluations cover chemical, biological, radiological and nuclear risk among the areas of potential catastrophic risk.** `ab-ev-claude37-card-published`
  undated
  _Date:_ The card states no publication date. Its references are dated February 2025, and the PDF was served from assets.anthropic.com on 2026-10-28; no date is asserted.
  _Limits:_ Publication of the card is not evidence that the evaluations it describes were carried out as described. The card carries no date of its own, so none is recorded.

  > “The RSP requires comprehensive safety evaluations prior to releasing frontier models in key areas of potential catastrophic risk: Chemical, Biological, Radiological, and Nuclear (CBRN); cybersecurity; and autonomous capabilities.”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 5, §1 Introduction, paragraph on the Responsible Scaling Policy

- **The Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card states that Anthropic does not currently run specific evaluations on chemical risk directly because it prioritises biological risks, and that it informs its views through chemical risk analysis performed by the UK and US AI institutes.** `ab-ev-claude37-chemical-not-evaluated`
  undated · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The card states no date; the statement describes practice at the time of the card.
  _Limits:_ A statement of the card's own evaluation scope at the time it was written. It is not evidence about any later practice, and the reliance it describes is not evidence that the institutes' analysis was performed or adopted.

  > “We do not currently run specific evaluations on Chemical Risk directly, since we prioritize biological risks.”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 24, §7.1.1 On Chemical Risks

- **The Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card states that in one uplift study models provided some amount of uplift to novices compared with participants who did not have access to a model, and that even the highest scoring plan from a participant with model access still included critical mistakes that would lead to failure in the real world.** `ab-ev-claude37-uplift-study-result`
  undated · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The card gives no date for the study or for its own publication.
  _Limits:_ One study as the developer reports it, for the models and participants it describes. The record does not say how large any uplift was, does not generalise it to other systems or settings, and carries the card's own statement that plans still failed on critical points.
  > “in one uplift study, we found that models provided some amount of uplift to novices compared to other participants who did not have access to a model”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 23, §7.1 CBRN Evaluations, 'Results' paragraph
  > “even the highest scoring plan from the participant who had access to a model still included critical mistakes that would lead to failure in the "real world".”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 23, §7.1, 'Results' paragraph, following sentence · context
  > “how the model performs on expert-level evaluations may not directly translate to real-world capabilities”  
  > [Claude 3.7 Sonnet System Card](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/785e231869ea8b3b/original/claude-3-7-sonnet-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 23, §7.1, 'Results' paragraph · context

### GPT-4 System Card

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **The GPT-4 System Card states that OpenAI subjected the model to stress testing, boundary testing and red teaming in four dual-use domains, biological weapons among them, to explore whether its models could provide information to proliferators.** `ab-ev-gpt4-card-proliferation-category`
  undated
  _Date:_ The card states no publication or revision date, and the dataset records its date as unknown; the expression read was retrieved on 2026-10-28.
  _Limits:_ Records the evaluation category the card names, not a finding that GPT-4 had any such capability, and not that the evaluations were adequate.

  > “We subjected the model to stress testing, boundary testing, and red teaming … in four dual-use domains to explore whether our models could provide the necessary information to proliferators … seeking to develop, acquire, or disperse nuclear, radiological, biological, and chemical weapons.”  
  > [GPT-4 System Card](https://cdn.openai.com/papers/gpt-4-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 12 (printed p. 52), §2.6 Proliferation of Conventional and Unconventional Weapons, first paragraph

- **The GPT-4 System Card states that red teamers put a set of questions to both GPT-4 and traditional search engines and found that the time to research completion was reduced when using GPT-4, while stating that access to GPT-4 on its own is an insufficient condition for proliferation.** `ab-ev-gpt4-card-search-comparison-result`
  undated · proposed predicate `states_evaluation_result`
  _Date:_ The card states no date for the publication or for the red-teaming period.
  _Limits:_ The result is the card's own, for the red teamers' question set and the comparison it describes. It is not generalised to other systems, later versions or real-world risk, and the card itself says the work was not intended to assess the probability that anyone would use the model for such purposes.
  > “Red teamers selected a set of questions to prompt both GPT-4 and traditional search engines, finding that the time to research completion was reduced when using GPT-4.”  
  > [GPT-4 System Card](https://cdn.openai.com/papers/gpt-4-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 12 (printed p. 52), §2.6, second paragraph
  > “On its own, access to GPT-4 is an insufficient condition for proliferation but could alter the information available to proliferators”  
  > [GPT-4 System Card](https://cdn.openai.com/papers/gpt-4-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 12 (printed p. 52), §2.6, second paragraph
  > “The red team assessed the model's capabilities but their work was not intended to assess the probability or likelihood of a user accessing the model for the purpose of developing unconventional weapons.”  
  > [GPT-4 System Card](https://cdn.openai.com/papers/gpt-4-system-card.pdf), PDF p. 12 (printed p. 52), §2.6, second paragraph, closing sentence · context

### Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **The United States Department of Health and Human Services, through its Office of the Secretary, published the Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA in the Federal Register of 13 October 2010, stating that it sets out recommended baseline standards for the gene and genome synthesis industry and other providers regarding the screening of orders.** `ab-scr-hhs-2010-published`
  2010-10-13 · published
  _Date:_ Federal Register issue of 13 October 2010 (75 FR 62820). The notice's DATES caption states the same day as the Guidance's effective date, which is recorded separately.
  _Limits:_ Records the publication and the notice's own description of what the Guidance sets out. Publication is not evidence that any provider screened an order or followed the Guidance. The notice also publishes the Government's responses to comments on the 2009 draft, which are not recorded.
  > “sets forth recommended baseline standards for the gene and genome synthesis industry and other providers of synthetic dsDNA products regarding the screening of orders”  
  > [Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA (75 FR 62820)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2010-10-13/pdf/2010-25728.pdf), SUMMARY, 75 FR 62820

#### Versions and changes

- **The 2023 Screening Framework Guidance for Providers and Users of Synthetic Nucleic Acids states that advances in the field motivated the United States Government to review and revise the 2010 Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA, and cites the 2010 guidance at 75 FR 62820.** `ab-scr-hhs-2023-supersedes-2010`
  2023-10-13 · published
  _Date:_ Dated by the publication of the revising expression.
  _Limits:_ The publisher's own statement that the later guidance revises the earlier one. The 2010 text and its history remain on record; what changed is recorded only where this pass states a provision of each expression.
  > “These advances and others motivated the U.S. government to review and revise the 2010 Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA.”  
  > [Screening Framework Guidance for Providers and Users of Synthetic Nucleic Acids (88 FR 70998)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-10-13/pdf/2023-22540.pdf), I. Introduction, 88 FR 70999

#### What the text states

- **The 2010 notice states that the final Guidance is directed to all providers of synthetic double-stranded DNA, the recommendations of the draft Guidance having been directed to 'commercial' providers.** `ab-scr-hhs-2010-all-providers-addressees`
  2010-10-13 · published
  _Date:_ Provision as the 2010 expression states it, dated by its publication in the Federal Register of 13 October 2010.
  _Limits:_ Records whom the notice says the final Guidance is directed to, and the change it states from the draft. It is not a finding that any provider, commercial or otherwise, screened an order, and the notice's further remark that 'orders' does not necessarily imply a commercial transaction is not recorded as a separate provision. Split under the one-claim-per-edge rule from ab-scr-hhs-2010-voluntary-all-providers. The provision kind 'threshold' is the kind the dataset uses for a definition that decides who is covered; see openQuestions.

  > “the final Guidance is directed to all providers of synthetic dsDNA”  
  > [Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA (75 FR 62820)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2010-10-13/pdf/2010-25728.pdf), Response to comments on scope, 75 FR 62823

- **The 2010 notice states that the Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA is effective on 13 October 2010.** `ab-scr-hhs-2010-effective-date`
  2010-10-13 · effective
  _Date:_ The effective date stated in the notice's DATES caption, which is also the date of the Federal Register issue.
  _Limits:_ An effective date stated for guidance whose observance the same notice describes as voluntary; it is not a compliance date for any provider and creates no obligation recorded here. The provision kind is `threshold`, the kind the dataset uses for a stated date of effect or application (`omni-entry-into-force`, `ny-effective-date`); the schema has no `date` kind.

  > “The Guidance is effective on October 13, 2010.”  
  > [Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA (75 FR 62820)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2010-10-13/pdf/2010-25728.pdf), DATES, 75 FR 62820

- **The 2010 Guidance states that following it is voluntary, though many of its specific recommendations serve to remind providers of their obligations under existing regulations.** `ab-scr-hhs-2010-voluntary-all-providers`
  2010-10-13 · published
  _Date:_ Provision as the 2010 expression states it, dated by its publication in the Federal Register of 13 October 2010.
  _Limits:_ The notice's statement about its own standing, not a finding about any provider's practice; the existing regulations it refers to are not recorded here. Split under the one-claim-per-edge rule from the record that also carried the Guidance's addressees, which are now the separate record ab-scr-hhs-2010-all-providers-addressees. The provision kind is the nearest the dataset schema admits (the schema has no kind for an instrument's standing): the sentence states that the Guidance itself imposes no duty beyond obligations existing regulations already carry. See openQuestions.
  > “Following this Guidance is voluntary, though many specific recommendations serve to remind providers of their obligations under existing regulations.”  
  > [Screening Framework Guidance for Providers of Synthetic Double-Stranded DNA (75 FR 62820)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2010-10-13/pdf/2010-25728.pdf), SUMMARY, 75 FR 62820

### S. 2399 (118th Congress): Artificial Intelligence and Biosecurity Risk Assessment Act, as introduced in the Senate

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **S. 2399 of the 118th Congress, the Artificial Intelligence and Biosecurity Risk Assessment Act, was introduced in the Senate on 19 July 2023 by Senator Edward J. Markey, with Senator Ted Budd as an original cosponsor, and read twice and referred to the Committee on Health, Education, Labor, and Pensions.** `ab-scr-s2399-introduced`
  2023-07-19 · undefined
  _Date:_ Introduction date in the official bill-status record and in the bill's own dateline. The status record as served on 28 October 2026 lists no later action than the referral of 19 July 2023. The date is the date of introduction; a status record carries no date basis, as the dataset’s own legislative status records do not.
  _Limits:_ Introduction is not passage or enactment, and the status record shows no action after the referral. The record names H.R. 4704 as a related bill, identified by the Congressional Research Service; that House bill is not separately recorded in this pass.
  > “Read twice and referred to the Committee on Health, Education, Labor, and Pensions.”  
  > [Bill status record for S. 2399 (118th Congress), Artificial Intelligence and Biosecurity Risk Assessment Act](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLSTATUS-118s2399/xml/BILLSTATUS-118s2399.xml), introducedDate, sponsors, cosponsors and actions elements

#### What the text states

- **S. 2399 as introduced would amend section 2811 of the Public Health Service Act to require the Assistant Secretary for Preparedness and Response to conduct risk assessments and implement strategic initiatives or activities to address whether technical advancements in artificial intelligence, such as open-source models and large language models, can be used intentionally or unintentionally to develop novel pathogens, viruses, bioweapons or chemical weapons, and provides that such initiatives and activities may include monitoring and research on potential global biological catastrophic risks and a summary of the risk assessment in the National Health Security Strategy.** `ab-scr-s2399-ai-risk-assessment`
  2023-07-19 · published
  _Date:_ Provision of the bill as introduced, dated by the publication of the introduced print of 19 July 2023 (the dateline of the print and the date in its govinfo package metadata, with the official bill-status record cited below); introduction in the Senate is the event behind that date. The bill-status record lists no later text version.
  _Limits:_ A bill as introduced states a proposal, not law: no duty is imposed, nothing in the two listed items is required of the Assistant Secretary even if the bill were enacted, and the bill did not advance beyond committee referral on the record inspected. The proposition is about what the bill would require, not about whether AI can be so used.
  > “artificial intelligence, such as open-source artificial intelligence models and large language models, can be used intentionally or unintentionally to develop novel pathogens, viruses, bioweapons, or chemical weapons”  
  > [S. 2399, Artificial Intelligence and Biosecurity Risk Assessment Act, as introduced in the Senate (118th Congress)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLS-118s2399is/xml/BILLS-118s2399is.xml), Sec. 2, adding section 2811(h) to the Public Health Service Act
  > the subsection provides that such initiatives and activities may include (1) regularly monitoring and researching potential global biological catastrophic risks and (2) including in the National Health Security Strategy a summary of the risk assessment conducted under the subsection. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [S. 2399, Artificial Intelligence and Biosecurity Risk Assessment Act, as introduced in the Senate (118th Congress)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLS-118s2399is/xml/BILLS-118s2399is.xml), Sec. 2, adding section 2811(h)(1) and (2)
  > the official bill-status record gives introducedDate 2023-07-19 and lists one text version, 'Introduced in Senate', dated 2023-07-19. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Bill status record for S. 2399 (118th Congress), Artificial Intelligence and Biosecurity Risk Assessment Act](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLSTATUS-118s2399/xml/BILLSTATUS-118s2399.xml), introducedDate and textVersions elements

#### What the text states

  _Limits:_ A proposal in a bill as introduced; it binds no purchaser and no provider. The enforcement sentence of the bill cross-refers to subsection (b)(2)(E) of the amended section, which concerns fees; the cross-reference is reproduced as the bill has it and is not interpreted.

### Screening Framework Guidance for Providers and Users of Synthetic Nucleic Acids

#### Issue, adoption and status

- **The Administration for Strategic Preparedness and Response published the Screening Framework Guidance for Providers and Users of Synthetic Nucleic Acids in the Federal Register of 13 October 2023, stating that it sets out baseline standards for the gene and genome synthesis industry and best practices for all entities involved in the provision, use and transfer of synthetic nucleic acids.** `ab-scr-hhs-2023-published`
  2023-10-13 · published

  > “sets forth baseline standards for the gene and genome synthesis industry, as well as best practices for all entities involved in the … provision, use, and transfer of synthetic nucleic acids, regarding screening orders and recipients and maintaining records”  
  > [Screening Framework Guidance for Providers and Users of Synthetic Nucleic Acids (88 FR 70998)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-10-13/pdf/2023-22540.pdf), SUMMARY, 88 FR 70998

#### What the text states

- **The 2023 Guidance recommends that manufacturers of benchtop nucleic acid synthesis equipment screen all customers purchasing such equipment to validate customer legitimacy, provide equipment only to customers with mechanisms to ensure that devices are operated by legitimate users, and integrate into the equipment the capability to screen sequences and to authenticate legitimate users.** `ab-scr-hhs-2023-benchtop-manufacturers`

  > “Manufacturers should screen all Customers purchasing benchtop nucleic acid synthesizers to validate customer legitimacy and that the equipment is appropriate for their needs.”

  > manufacturers should integrate into benchtop nucleic acid synthesizers the capability to screen sequences and to authenticate legitimate users, at a level the Guidance describes as on a par with the screening practices it recommends for providers. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [Screening Framework Guidance for Providers and Users of Synthetic Nucleic Acids (88 FR 70998)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-10-13/pdf/2023-22540.pdf), IV. Best practices for benchtop equipment, later bullet, 88 FR 71001

- **The 2023 Guidance recommends that, upon receiving an order for synthetic nucleic acids, providers and third-party vendors perform sequence screening, verify the identity of their customers, and follow up to verify the legitimacy of the order when sequences in the Guidance's category of concern are identified.** `ab-scr-hhs-2023-provider-screening`

### Executive Order 14110: Safe, Secure, and Trustworthy Development and Use of Artificial Intelligence

#### What the text states

- **Section 4.4(b)(i) of Executive Order 14110 directs the Director of the Office of Science and Technology Policy, within 180 days and in consultation with named agency heads, to establish a framework encouraging providers of synthetic nucleic acid sequences to implement comprehensive, scalable and verifiable procurement screening mechanisms, including standards and recommended incentives.** `ab-scr-eo14110-sec44b-ostp-framework`
  2023-11-01 · published
  _Date:_ Provision as the edition states it, dated by the Federal Register publication of 1 November 2023; the order is signed 30 October 2023. Executive Order 14110 was revoked by Executive Order 14148 of 20 January 2025.
  _Limits:_ States what the order directs; it does not establish that the framework was produced, nor impose any duty on a provider. The order's own criteria for identifying sequences that could pose a risk, in subparagraph (A), and its direction on screening methodologies, in subparagraph (B), are not recorded. The dataset already holds this order's Sec. 3(k) definition and Sec. 4.2 reporting provisions; Sec. 4.4 was not recorded there.

  > “To reduce the risk of misuse of synthetic nucleic acids, which could be substantially increased by AI's capabilities in this area, and improve biosecurity measures for the nucleic acid synthesis industry”  
  > [Executive Order 14110 of October 30, 2023: Safe, Secure, and Trustworthy Development and Use of Artificial Intelligence (88 FR 75191)](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-11-01/pdf/2023-24283.pdf), Sec. 4.4(b), opening words, 88 FR 75201 · context

  _Limits:_ Records what the guidance states as its background. It is not a finding that AI increases biological risk, and the guidance cites no evaluation or study for the statement in this passage. The provision kind is `risk_domain`, because the passage names the risk the guidance addresses rather than a duty; the schema has no `definition` kind.

  > “Increased access to synthetic nucleic acids, coupled with developments in converging tools and technologies – such as AI – and an increase in knowledge and expertise, may exacerbate the risks of engineering biology.”  
  > [UK screening guidance on synthetic nucleic acids for users and providers](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/uk-screening-guidance-on-synthetic-nucleic-acids/uk-screening-guidance-on-synthetic-nucleic-acids-for-users-and-providers), Background section

- **The UK screening guidance states that providers and third-party vendors should know and document who they are distributing to, should verify the identity of the customer on receiving an order for synthetic nucleic acids, and should retain customer screening information and ask the customer to update it at least every 18 months.** `ab-scr-uk-guidance-customer-screening`

  _Date:_ Provision as the published expression states it, dated by its publication on GOV.UK.
  _Limits:_ States what the guidance recommends. It is not evidence that any provider verifies customers or holds such records, and the guidance's list of information to be collected and reviewed is not reproduced here.

  > “Providers should retain customer screening information and refer to it for repeat orders. At least every 18 months the provider should ask the customer to update their information.”  
  > [UK screening guidance on synthetic nucleic acids for users and providers](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/uk-screening-guidance-on-synthetic-nucleic-acids/uk-screening-guidance-on-synthetic-nucleic-acids-for-users-and-providers), Customer screening, providers and third-party vendors
  > providers and third-party vendors should know and document who they are distributing to and, on receiving an order for synthetic nucleic acids, should verify the identity of the customer, following the guidance's customer screening methodology. _(paraphrase)_  
  > [UK screening guidance on synthetic nucleic acids for users and providers](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/uk-screening-guidance-on-synthetic-nucleic-acids/uk-screening-guidance-on-synthetic-nucleic-acids-for-users-and-providers), Customer screening, providers and third-party vendors, first bullets

'Expected standards of responsible practice' is the guidance's wording; it does not establish a legal obligation, and the separate statutory duties the guidance points to are recorded separately. Split under the one-claim-per-edge rule from the addressees, now the separate record ab-scr-uk-guidance-addressees. The provision kind 'required_action' is the nearest the schema admits for a statement of what practice an instrument expects; see openQuestions.

  > “This gene synthesis guidance sets out expected standards of responsible practice to address the current level of biosecurity risk associated with access to synthetic nucleic acid.”

- **The UK screening guidance states that where an order for synthetic nucleic acids contains a sequence in the guidance's category of concern that is listed in Schedule 5 of the Anti-terrorism, Crime and Security Act 2001, the provider must ask the user to provide proof that they have notified the Home Office under Part 7 of that Act, and that where such a sequence derives from an animal pathogen in Groups 3 or 4 of Schedule 1 of the Specified Animal Pathogens Order the provider must ask for proof of a licence from the Health and Safety Executive.** `ab-scr-uk-guidance-statutory-notification`
