## Open questions for the maintainer

- The proposition codes B1 to B7 used here clash with the dataset's existing codes B1 to B6 (deceptive alignment, the treacherous turn, strategic concealment, scheming and two others). Schema validation rejects duplicate proposition codes, so the pack's codes cannot be admitted as written. _Recommended:_ Keep B1–B7 inside the pack, where the brief set them, and re-letter them on admission to an unused letter — D1–D7 would be free (the dataset uses A, B, C, E, G, M and Y). The integrator should decide the letter once, for the whole pack, and record the mapping in the pack README. (arguments)
- Every proposition here carries an empty `terms` list, so no record holds an earliest-located-use-of-a-name role. "Uplift" is a usual name for B1 in these texts and appears in two recorded passages. _Recommended:_ Register "uplift" as B1's usual name only together with an earliest-located-term record, and search for its earliest located use separately; schema validation requires exactly one such record once a term is listed. Until then leaving `terms` empty is the honest state. (arguments)
- Three propositions (B3, B4, B7) have no contesting text in this draft. For B3 that is because a text asserting that current evaluations do measure real-world risk was not located; for B4 and B7 no located text disputes the distinction or the scope. _Recommended:_ Treat the absence as a search result, not a finding, and leave it as it is. If the maintainer wants it on the record, a bounded negative record per proposition would need its own search terms and scope, which this pass did not run. (arguments)
- Anthropic's post is both a developer's argument and a report of its own trials. This pass records only its methodological and argument-level statements, while the `evaluations` pass may record its result. _Recommended:_ Keep the split, and have the integrator check that no evaluation result is recorded twice across the two drafts. Cite the same source key (`anthropic-biorisk`) in both so the quotation budget for that page is counted once. (arguments)
- Sandbrink's preprint was revised eight times between June and December 2023, and the abstract wording changed; this draft cites v1 only. _Recommended:_ Keep v1 as the dated expression for the earliest-located roles. If a later version is wanted for the argument's developed form, record it as a separate document version rather than editing these records. (arguments)
- The RAND working paper (Brent and McKelvey) is marked by its publisher as not formally reviewed or edited, and a later RAND perspective with a similar title (PEA3853-1) was seen but not retrieved. _Recommended:_ Keep the working paper as the dated expression and record the perspective separately if a later pass retrieves it; do not merge them into one document. (arguments)
- Proposition B2 has no articulating record after the correction of ab-arg-b2-batalis-articulates, so its earliest-located articulation role has no holder. The earliest located text on B2 is now the contesting record ab-arg-b2-soice-contests (6 June 2023). _Recommended:_ Leave the role without a holder rather than promoting a text that does not meet the criterion; schema validation requires a holder only where an articulation exists, and schema 2.16's `withheld` list is for a role withheld from existing articulations. A later pass should search for an articulation of B2 — the paywalled venues listed in `howWeSearched` are the obvious gap — and record it with the role if one is found. (arguments)
- ab-arg-b2-batalis-articulates now holds a stance its id does not name. Ids are not yet stable (the pack is unmerged), but this one is cited in research/ai-bio/audit/audit-A.json, verification.json, audit-sample.json, pack.md and cross-family-input-2026-10-28.md. _Recommended:_ Keep the id for now, so the finding and its correction stay traceable, and rename it to ab-arg-b2-batalis-bears-on at admission, when pack.json is rebuilt and the references can be updated in one step. (arguments)
- The audit sampled four of the records whose publication date is attested only by a source that was not cited as evidence. The same gap remains in the records it did not sample: ab-arg-b1-soice-articulates and ab-arg-b5-soice-articulates (arxiv-2306-03809-abs), ab-arg-b5-sandbrink-articulates and ab-arg-b7-sandbrink-articulates (arxiv-2306-13952-abs), ab-arg-b3-brent-articulates (arxiv-2506-13798-abs), ab-arg-b4-batalis-articulates and ab-arg-b7-batalis-bears-on (cset-biorisk-explainer), and ab-arg-b1-mouton-contests (rand-mouton-2024-page). _Recommended:_ Apply the same correction to those eight records in a separately authorised pass: one evidence item per record citing the listing or landing page for the date, as the corrected records now do. The budgets allow it; each of those five sources is used for dates only and has its full allowance left. This pass changed only the records the auditors named. (arguments)
- ab-arg-b1-soice-articulates rests on the same sentence as ab-arg-b2-soice-contests and drops the same condition ("as soon as they are credibly identified"), and it carries the source's own category term where the B2 record substitutes a neutral paraphrase. Neither auditor sampled it. _Recommended:_ Restore the condition there too, and replace the category term with the neutral paraphrase used in ab-arg-b2-soice-contests, in the same separately authorised pass. It was left unchanged here because no finding covered it. (arguments)
- The support role of the evidence is used against the proposition rather than against the record: every contesting record in this draft carries only `support: "contradicts"` passages, and the one bears_on record carries only `context`. The dataset uses `supports` for the passage that shows the text contesting or bearing on the proposition (all 213 `contests_proposition` and all 293 `bears_on_proposition` claims in data/claims.json), and schema validation requires at least one supporting item on a supported record (src/domain/schema.ts, "Supported and contested claims require supporting evidence"). Neither auditor raised it. _Recommended:_ Before admission, relabel those passages `supports` — the support role describes the evidence's relation to the record, and the record is that the text contests or bears on the proposition — and keep `contradicts` for a passage that tells against the record itself. Seven records still have no supporting item: ab-arg-b1-mouton-contests, ab-arg-b1-peppin-contests, ab-arg-b1-itif-contests, ab-arg-b2-anthropic-contests, ab-arg-b5-moulange-contests, ab-arg-b6-kapoor-contests and ab-arg-b7-batalis-bears-on. The corrections of 2026-10-28 incidentally gave ab-arg-b2-soice-contests, ab-arg-b2-brent-contests and ab-arg-b2-batalis-articulates a supporting date item, which is not a disposition of this question. Nothing was relabelled here, since it is outside the findings this pass applied. (arguments)
- Should `states_evaluation_result` enter the schema as a predicate, and should its `attributes.evaluation` block (what, how, result, caveats) be added to the claim schema? _Recommended:_ Yes, if any of this pack is admitted: 24 of the 49 records here use it, and no existing predicate carries a report's stated result with the report's own caveats. Keep `reports_evaluation_of` for naming an evaluation category, as the dataset already does. (evaluations)
- `evaluation_engagement` allows `organisation` subjects and objects only (`src/domain/predicates.ts`, enforced by the schema's subject-type check), but the UK institute is typed `public_body` in the dataset (`uk-ai-security-institute`), so the two UK engagement records here — `ab-ev-gpt5-uk-aisi-engagement` and `ab-ev-claude37-uk-aisi-engagement` — cannot be admitted as structured. Auditor A raised this on the first of the two; the second has the identical defect and was not separately audited. Neither record was changed, because the pack changes neither the dataset nor the schema. _Recommended:_ A maintainer decision is needed before either record is admitted. Preferred: widen the predicate's subjects to include `public_body` rather than retyping the institute, since the existing US records use `us-ai-safety-institute`, which is typed `organisation`, so the dataset is already inconsistent on this point. The alternative, if the predicate is to stay as it is, is to re-record each engagement from the document's side (subject the system card, with the institute named in the statement), which loses the engagement relation and so is worse for a reader. Whichever is chosen, `ab-ev-gpt5-uk-aisi-engagement` carries the limit in its own qualification and `ab-ev-claude37-uk-aisi-engagement` should be given the same wording when the decision is applied. (evaluations)
- The NIST summary page for the upgraded Claude 3.5 Sonnet carries the footer 'Released November 19, 2024, Updated March 20, 2025', the same mutable-source problem auditor B found on the o1 page. Widening that finding to the affected source family, as the editorial policy requires, has now been applied to the Claude 3.5 Sonnet records (`ab-ev-usaisi-claude35-bio-result`, `ab-ev-usaisi-claude35-tooling-pilot`, `ab-ev-usaisi-anthropic-claude35-engagement`) and the `ab-nist-preeval-claude35` source entry, and to the second record on the o1 page, the o1 System Card PDF (whose served file carries a footnote dated 12/19/2024) and the Grok 4 Model Card; the entries are in `auditCorrections`. Nothing is left open on this page. _Recommended:_ Applied as recommended: each record's `time.note` says that the expression checked is the copy served on 2026-10-28, whose footer gives an update date of 20 March 2025, with the release date of 19 November 2024 kept as the record's date, and the source entry's `version` field says the same. The footer string was verified in the cache on 2026-10-28; no date was changed. What remains for a checker who can reach the Internet Archive is to compare the released and updated expressions of both NIST pages, which neither page describes. (evaluations)
- Developers' classification decisions (treating a launch as High capability, applying ASL-3) are recorded here as stated evaluation results. Should they instead be `states_provision` records against the framework edition, or a separate predicate? _Recommended:_ Keep them as stated results of the capability assessment, since each is the stated outcome of evaluations rather than a provision of a framework text, and the framework provisions are the frameworks pass's business. If the maintainer prefers, a `states_capability_determination` predicate would separate them cleanly. (evaluations)
- Three sources are mutable or undated: Anthropic's transparency hub, the Gemini 3 Pro card ('Last Updated: May 2026') and the Claude 3.7 Sonnet card (no date at all). Should undated developer documents be admitted at all? _Recommended:_ Admit them with the treatment used here: a snapshot date for the mutable page, the stated last-update date for the card that gives one, and `time.kind: unknown` where the document states no date. Ask the cross-family checker to re-read the transparency hub, since its wording may have changed. (evaluations)
- The o1 card was read as a PDF expression (cdn.openai.com, file name dated 20241205) while the dataset source `source-o1-card` records the web page. Should these be one source or two? _Recommended:_ Treat them as two expressions of one document but share the quotation budget, as done here, until someone compares the two copies word for word. (evaluations)
- Should the pack add the earlier RAND document of October 2023 ('A Red-Team Approach') and record the relation between the two? _Recommended:_ Yes, if the screening or arguments pass retrieves it; the January 2024 report is the one that states results, so the pack loses nothing substantive without it. (evaluations)
- The `BASES` set in research/ai-bio/build.mjs has no `revision`, but the dataset dates the xAI December 2025 edition and the Microsoft February 2026 edition by their revision dates, with `basis: "revision"`. Six records in this draft use that basis and will be reported as problems by `tools.mjs merge`. _Recommended:_ Add `revision` to `BASES` in research/ai-bio/build.mjs. Keeping date fidelity matters more than the tool’s current list, and relabelling a revision date as a publication date would assert a publication that neither edition establishes. (frameworks)
- Should the Meta and xAI June 2026 editions be left without records in this pack, or should the cross-family checker be asked to retrieve the three unreachable expressions and draft their records? _Recommended:_ Ask the cross-family checker to open the three Internet Archive captures named in retrievalFailures and to report the biological provisions of each edition. Admitting the pack with three of the 22 editions unread should be stated as a coverage limit rather than left implicit. (frameworks)
- Four of the retrieved PDFs (Responsible Scaling Policy v2.2, v3.1, v3.3, v3.4) have text layers that displace the first character of many lines, and three more (Preparedness Framework Beta p. 9, Preparedness Framework Version 2 Table 1, Frontier Governance Framework 2026 tables) interleave table columns. Verbatim quotes from those passages cannot be checked mechanically, so this draft uses explicit paraphrases with locators there. _Recommended:_ Keep the paraphrases. If a quote from those passages is wanted later, extract each table column separately, as the dataset record absent-rsp-v3-3-no-train-deploy-without-safeguards says was done, and record which extraction was checked. (frameworks)
- Several sources have almost no quotation budget left: source-fgf-2026 has 1 word of headroom and source-ms-fgf-2026 has 4, against the editorial policy’s per-source limit. Three records in this draft rest on paraphrase for that reason alone. _Recommended:_ Accept the paraphrases. If the exact wording of the Frontier Governance Framework tiers or the Microsoft 2026 CBRN rows is later wanted as a quote, it needs a maintainer decision on which existing dataset quote from the same source to retire. (frameworks)
- Should the Anthropic analogy between AI Safety Levels and US biosafety level standards be recorded, and under which provision kind? _Recommended:_ Record it if the maintainer wants the framing in the corpus, as a `states_provision` of kind `threshold` on rsp-2023 with an explicit qualification that it records the derivation of the scheme’s name and no provision about biological risk. It was left out of this draft because no schema provision kind fits it cleanly. (frameworks)
- The provision keys in this draft (for example `rsp-cbrn-4-threshold`, `fsf-cbrn-uplift-1-threshold`, `pf-bio-chem-high-threshold`) are new and align editions within one family only. _Recommended:_ Keep them family-scoped, as the dataset’s existing keys are. Aligning thresholds across developers is a comparison question (the `comparison-*` keys) and should not be done by reusing a provision key. (frameworks)
- ab-fw-rsp-v3-2-novel-cb-threat-pathway-sharing cannot carry the verbatim quote that schema validation requires of a stated provision: the cell’s sentence is 26 words as quoted for v3.0, and source-rsp-3-2 has 22 words of headroom (393 quoted of a limit of 415). The audit of 2026-10-28 asked for the quote; it was declined for that reason. _Recommended:_ Admit the record with an elided quote of the sentence’s outer limbs (“we will identify the most concerning specific threat pathways … and share this content with policymakers”, 16 counted words, within the 22 available), keeping the paraphrase beside it for the middle limb. Retiring one of the dataset’s 16 existing quotes from source-rsp-3-2 to free the budget would also work but is a maintainer decision. Do not drop the record: the same provision is recorded for v3.0 with a quote, and omitting it for v3.2 would leave a hole in the edition series. (frameworks)
- Auditor A asks, for all three Biological Weapons Convention records (ab-gov-bwc-us-wp14-ai-verification, ab-gov-bwc-uk-wp27-biotech-ai-threats, ab-gov-bwc-japan-wp28-misuse-risk), whether states_provision suits a national statement at all: the predicate's description is about an exact framework edition stating one provision. _Recommended:_ Decide before admission. A national statement is not an instrument's provision; the honest options are a new predicate for a position stated in a document submitted to a treaty meeting, or recording these as propositions in the arguments strand with the document as the text that articulates them. The three records now use provision kind 'risk_domain' consistently, which keeps them readable either way, and none of them says that any State Party acted. (measures)
- Is the national security memorandum of 24 October 2024 still in force? Executive Order 14179 of 23 January 2025 directs a review of actions taken pursuant to Executive Order 14110, which the memorandum says it fulfils, but no revocation or replacement of the memorandum was retrieved in this pass. _Recommended:_ Record the memorandum's provisions as stated and dated, with no status beyond issuance (as done here), and task a short follow-up search of 2025-2026 presidential documents and whitehouse.gov for a replacing national security memorandum before any admission. Do not infer revocation from the revocation of Executive Order 14110. (measures)
- How should the NIST centre named on the page retrieved here (Center for Advancing Innovation and Standards for Super Intelligence, CAISSI) relate to the Center for AI Standards and Innovation (CAISI) and the U.S. AI Safety Institute already in the dataset? _Recommended:_ Keep them as separate identifiers until a naming announcement is retrieved, and record any link with the name_transition_announced predicate in a separate task. This pack's record cites only what the page states about the centre's evaluation focus. (measures)
- BWC/MSP/2025/WP.4 carries 10 December 2025 on its face but a submit date of 21 April 2026 in the UNODA documents list. Which date should a record use? _Recommended:_ Date the record by the document's own date (as done here) and keep the list's submit date in the document's dateNote, since the document date is the expression's own and the list date may reflect UN processing. (measures)
- Should national statements delivered at a Biological Weapons Convention meeting and issued as working papers (WP.14, WP.27, WP.28 here) be recorded as documents of the submitting State Party, with a published or signed_commitment claim, rather than only as provision records on the document? _Recommended:_ Record them as document versions with states_provision claims, as here, because the available predicates for publication take an organisation or public body as subject and this pack creates no country entities. If the maintainer wants attribution to States Parties, add country entities and a publication predicate in a later task. (measures)
- Two records in this draft are bounded negative records about documents that the dataset already treats as central (Executive Order 14409 and the EU AI Act). Are they wanted in a candidate pack? _Recommended:_ Keep both: each states its search terms, scope and date, and both answer a research question this pack poses (where biological risk is and is not named in current instruments). They are flagged for the cross-family check as inferred and negative records. (measures)
- The schema-fit defects the auditors found in the sampled records run through records they did not sample, which this task left untouched. Provision kinds the schema does not admit: 'date' in ab-scr-hhs-2010-effective-date, ab-scr-ostp-framework-funding-requirement-effective, ab-scr-aspr-pages-framework-revision-pending and ab-scr-ostp-framework-replacement-not-found; 'definition' in ab-scr-hhs-2023-scope, ab-scr-ostp-framework-scope and ab-scr-uk-guidance-ai-in-background; 'enforcement' in ab-scr-s2400-federal-purchase-restriction. Bases the schema does not admit: 'introduction' in ab-scr-hr4702-introduced, ab-scr-s2399-introduced and ab-scr-s2400-federal-purchase-restriction. _Recommended:_ Fix them in one authorised pass, the way the audited records were fixed here: drop the basis on a status record and say the kind of date in the note; on a provision record use the basis the source gives; and map 'date', 'definition' and 'enforcement' onto the kinds the schema has. This task applied only the audited findings, so the unsampled records are left as they stand rather than changed without a check. (screening)
- The schema's provision kinds have no entry for an instrument's standing (whether following it is voluntary or expected practice) or for whom it addresses. The audited records now use 'exception', 'required_action' and 'threshold' as the nearest admitted kinds. _Recommended:_ Decide at admission between adding a kind (for example 'standing' and 'scope') and keeping the nearest kinds with the qualification each record now carries. The dataset already uses 'threshold' for definitional provisions that decide who or what is covered, so the addressee records follow existing practice; the standing records do not have a precedent. (screening)
- This draft holds 45 records against the brief's aim of about 30. Keep them all, or trim? _Recommended:_ Keep. Each instrument needed its publication, scope, central requirement, dates and status. If the pack must be trimmed, drop in this order: ab-scr-hhs-2010-effective-date (the date survives in the document entry and the timeline), ab-scr-uk-guidance-website-statement, ab-scr-hr4702-introduced, ab-scr-eo14110-sec44bii-nist-engagement, ab-scr-ostp-framework-exceptions, ab-scr-igsc-hsp-v31-record-retention, ab-scr-hhs-2022-draft-published, ab-scr-igsc-member-directory-listing. That leaves 37. (screening)
- Should the pack record the minimum length of order subject to screening and the dates on which it changes, which three of these instruments state? _Recommended:_ No for the parameter, which is a screening criterion. If the maintainer wants the dates, a bounded record is possible that states only that each instrument sets a date from which a wider screening scope applies, and notes that the IGSC protocol gives 24 October 2026 where the OSTP framework gives 13 October 2026. I left both out and recorded the discrepancy in the omissions instead. (screening)
- Bill coverage stops at the 118th Congress because no full-text bill search was reachable. Should a later pass obtain access? _Recommended:_ Yes, as a bounded follow-up: either a congress.gov or govinfo API key in the environment's secrets, or the cross-family checker (who can reach the Internet Archive) asked to list bills of the 119th Congress whose text contains 'gene synthesis'. Until then the pack should say that its bill coverage is the 118th Congress items located. (screening)
- The OSTP framework's provisions are recorded from the copy marked 'Revised: September 2024', at month precision, because version 1 of April 2024 could not be retrieved. Is that acceptable? _Recommended:_ Yes, with the qualification as worded. Ask the cross-family checker to retrieve an Internet Archive capture of the framework from May to August 2024 and say whether the recorded provisions, in particular the attestation criterion and the 26 April 2025 date, stood in version 1. (screening)
- The framework's own text gives Executive Order 14110's date as 23 October 2023, where the order is dated 30 October 2023. _Recommended:_ Keep the quotation as printed and the qualification that records the discrepancy. Do not silently correct a quoted source. (screening)
- Should the pack record that the Administration for Strategic Preparedness and Response is a unit of the Department of Health and Human Services? _Recommended:_ Not in this pass. The two candidate entities are kept distinct and the relation is left unrecorded; `unit_of` would need its own evidence. (screening)
